# The corrected paper and its Lean formalization — tbs_prte_optimal_transport

For each result of the corrected paper: its statement, the Lean declaration that states it, whether that declaration is proved, and how the two statements correspond phrase by phrase. A result stated differently from the original paper is marked "corrected"; the report says what changed and why.

## Theorem 2.3

**Statement.** \label{thm:1d_ot} Let $\mu$ and $\nu$ be probability measures on $\mathbb{R}$ with quantile functions $Q_\mu$ and $Q_\nu$. Suppose either that $\mu$ has bounded support and $\nu$ has a finite first moment, or vice versa, and let the cost be the simple product $c(a,b) = a \cdot b$. Then, the minimum and maximum of the expected cost over all valid couplings are completely characterized by the countermonotonic and comonotonic couplings, respectively: \begin{align*} \min_{\pi \in \Pi(\mu, \nu)} \int_{\mathbb{R}^2} a \cdot b \, \mathrm{d}\pi(a, b) &= \int_0^1 Q_\mu(t) Q_\nu(1-t) \, \mathrm{d}t, \\ \max_{\pi \in \Pi(\mu, \nu)} \int_{\mathbb{R}^2} a \cdot b \, \mathrm{d}\pi(a, b) &= \int_0^1 Q_\mu(t) Q_\nu(t) \, \mathrm{d}t. \end{align*} The minimum is attained by the countermonotonic coupling $(Q_\mu(\xi), Q_\nu(1-\xi))$, and the maximum by the comonotonic coupling $(Q_\mu(\xi), Q_\nu(\xi))$, where $\xi \sim \mathrm{Unif}(0,1)$.

**Lean.** `Papers.TbsPrteOptimalTransport.productCost_transport_extrema` — `lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/T1dOt.lean:22`

**Status.** proved in Lean, under 2 condition(s) made explicit. **Axioms.** the standard ones only (propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound).

## Proposition 3.1

**Statement.** \label{prop:sharpness} Suppose the observed distribution $\mathbb{P}_{obs}$ is generated by a true structural model satisfying \cref{asp:structure}. Let $\tilde{\pi}$ be any probability measure on $\mathcal{Y} \times \mathcal{Y} \times [0,1] \times \mathcal{X}$ whose $(Y(w), U, X)$-marginals $\tilde{\pi}_w$ satisfy $(\tilde{\pi}_0, \tilde{\pi}_1) \in \Gamma(\mathbb{P}_{obs})$. Then there exists a joint distribution of the latent and observable variables $(Y(0), Y(1), U, X, Z, W)$ such that: \begin{enumerate} \item[(i)] the joint marginal distribution of $(Y(0), Y(1), U, X)$ is exactly $\tilde{\pi}$; \item[(ii)] \cref{asp:structure} holds for $(Y(0), Y(1), U, X, Z, W)$; \item[(iii)] the induced distribution of $(Z, X, W, Y)$ exactly matches $\mathbb{P}_{obs}$. \end{enumerate} Conversely, every joint marginal $\tilde{\pi}$ arising from a distribution of $(Y(0), Y(1), U, X, Z, W)$ satisfying \textup{(ii)}--\textup{(iii)} has $(Y(w), U, X)$-marginals in $\Gamma(\mathbb{P}_{obs})$ for $w \in \{0, 1\}$.

**Lean.** `Papers.TbsPrteOptimalTransport.feasible_iff_structural` — `lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/TSharpness.lean:25`

**Status.** proved in Lean, under 4 condition(s) made explicit. **Axioms.** the standard ones only (propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound).

- reading: $Y=WY(1)+(1-W)Y(0)$ almost surely under the structural population law. → `ConsistencyAEReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Basic.lean:177`)
- reading: $W=\indicator(U\leqslant p(Z,X))$ almost surely under the structural population law. → `ThresholdCrossingAEReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Basic.lean:182`)
- reading: $\mathcal L(U\mid X=x)=\mathrm{Unif}(0,1)$ for $\mathbb P_X$-almost every $x$. → `UniformResistanceAEReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Basic.lean:187`)
- reading: Every constraint in $\eqref{eq:ot_formulation}$ is imposed simultaneously for both $w\in\{0,1\}$. → `ConstraintsBothStatesReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Basic.lean:453`)
- condition of the paper: Throughout, we assume $Y(w) \in \mathcal{Y}$ almost surely. → `PotentialSupport` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Basic.lean:117`)
- condition of the paper: \item (Consistency) $Y = WY(1) + (1-W)Y(0)$. → `Consistency` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Basic.lean:124`)
- condition of the paper: \item (Conditional Instrumental Exogeneity) $Z \indep (U,Y(0),Y(1)) \mid X$, where $\indep$ denotes statistical independence. → `InstrumentExogeneity` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Basic.lean:131`)
- condition of the paper: \item (Threshold Crossing) The treatment is selected by \begin{align}\label{eq:thresh_xing} W = \indicator (U\leqslant p(Z,X)), \end{align} where $p(Z,X) = \mathbb{P}(W=1 \mid Z, X)$. → `ThresholdCrossing` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Basic.lean:142`)
- condition of the paper: Moreover, $U \mid X \sim \mathrm{Unif}(0,1)$. → `UniformResistance` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Basic.lean:154`)
- condition of the paper: \item (Common Instrument Support) The conditional support of $Z$ given $X$ does not depend on $X$: $\mathrm{Supp}(Z \mid X=x) = \mathcal{Z}$ for all $x \in \mathcal{X}$. → `CommonInstrumentSupport` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Basic.lean:161`)
- condition of the paper: Throughout the paper, we assume that all domains are compact, carry their Borel $\sigma$-fields, and are standard Borel spaces. → `DomainsCompactBorel` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Basic.lean:102`)
- definition of the paper: Let $\pi_w$ denote the joint probability measure of $(Y(w), U, X)$ and let $\pi_w(\cdot\mid x,u)$ denote the conditional distribution of $Y(w)$ given $x,u$. → `latentMarginal` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Basic.lean:271`)
- definition of the paper: so write $\mathcal{R}_1(p) \coloneqq (0,p]$ and $\mathcal{R}_0(p) \coloneqq (p,1]$ for the two selected regions. → `selRegion` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Basic.lean:278`)
- definition of the paper: \begin{equation}\label{eq:canonical_submeasures} H_{w,x}(p,\mathrm{d}y)\coloneqq \mathbb P_{\mathrm{obs}}(Y\in\mathrm{d}y,W=w\mid P=p,X=x), \qquad w\in\{0,1\}. \end{equation} → `obsSubmeasure` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Basic.lean:285`)
- definition of the paper: To select canonical versions at every point of the conditional propensity support, let $K_{w,x}(\mathrm{d}y\mid u)$ be a regular conditional law of $Y(w)$ given $(U,X)=(u,x)$. Under \cref{asp:structure}, the preceding derivation gives \[ H_{w,x}(p,\mathrm{d}y)=\int_{\mathcal{R}_w(p)} K_{w,x}(\mathrm{d}y\mid u)\,\mathrm{d}u, \qquad w\in\{0,1\}, \] for $P\mid X=x$-almost every $p$. The right-hand sides are Lipschitz in total variation and hence extend the observed equivalence classes in~(\ref{eq:canonical_submeasures}) continuously to $\operatorname{supp}(P\mid X=x)$. This extension is unique: two continuous versions that agree almost surely under the conditional law of $P$ agree everywhere on its support. We henceforth use $H_{w,x}$ for these canonical observed versions. Thus $H_{w,x}$ is identified from $\mathbb P_{\mathrm{obs}}$ on the conditional propensity support, including its endpoints even when the corresponding singleton events have probability zero; the latent kernels $K_{w,x}$ themselves need not be identified. With no covariates we write $H_w$. → `canonSubmeasure` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Basic.lean:309`)
- definition of the paper: \begin{equation}\label{eq:ot_formulation} \begin{split} \max_{\pi_0, \pi_1} \ / \min_{\pi_0, \pi_1}\quad & \theta_\omega(\pi_0, \pi_1)\\ \text{subject to} \quad & \int_{\mathcal{R}_w(p(z,x))} \pi_w(\mathrm{d}y \mid u, x) \mathrm{d}u = H_{w,x}(p(z,x),\mathrm{d}y), \quad \mathbb P_{Z,X}\text{-a.e. }(z,x),\\ & \pi_w(\mathrm{d}x, \mathrm{d}u) = \mathrm{d}u \ \mathbb{P}_{obs}(\mathrm{d}x),\\ & \pi_w \text{ is supported on } \mathcal{Y} \times [0,1] \times \mathcal{X}. \end{split} \end{equation} → `ccotValueSet` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Basic.lean:461`)
- definition of the paper: Let $\Gamma(\mathbb{P}_{obs})$ be the set of all measure pairs $(\pi_0, \pi_1)$ satisfying the constraints in (\ref{eq:ot_formulation}). → `IsFeasiblePair` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Basic.lean:433`)
- condition of the paper: \label{prop:sharpness} Suppose the observed distribution $\mathbb{P}_{obs}$ is generated by a true structural model satisfying \cref{asp:structure}. → `ObservedLawGenerated` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Basic.lean:238`)

## Theorem 3.4

**Statement.** \label{thm:discrete_lower_bound} Suppose \cref{asp:structure} and \cref{asp:target_weight} hold, $X$ is almost surely constant, and $\mathcal{Z}$ is finite. For $i=0, \dots, K-1$, let $Q_{Y, i}(t)$ be the quantile function of the identified conditional distribution $\mu_{1, i}$ defined in (\ref{eq:y_identified_measure}). Let $Q_{\omega, i}(t)$ be the quantile function of the random variable $\omega(U)$ where $U \sim \text{Unif}(p_i, p_{i+1})$. The sharp lower bound for the $W=1$ component of the PRTE is given by: \begin{equation}\label{eq:discrete_lower_bound} \begin{split} \underline{\theta}_{\omega, 1} = \ & \sum_{i=0}^{K-1} (p_{i+1} - p_i) \int_0^1 Q_{Y, i}(t) Q_{\omega, i}(1-t) \, \mathrm{d}t \\ & + \int_{p_K}^1 \Big( y_{\min} \max\{0, \omega(u)\} + y_{\max} \min\{0, \omega(u)\} \Big) \mathrm{d}u. \end{split} \end{equation} Similarly, the sharp upper bound is given by replacing $Q_{\omega,i}(1-t)$ with $Q_{\omega,i}(t)$ in the OT term and swapping $y_{\min}$ and $y_{\max}$ in the trivial tail term.

- definition of the paper: \begin{equation}\label{eq:ot_formulation_1} \begin{split} \min_{ \pi_1}\quad & \mathbb{E}_{\pi_1}[Y(1)\omega(X,U)]\\ \text{subject to} \quad & \int_0^{p(z,x)} \pi_1(\mathrm{d}y \mid u, x) \mathrm{d}u = H_{1,x}(p(z,x),\mathrm{d}y),\quad \mathbb P_{Z,X}\text{-a.e. }(z,x)\\ & \pi_1(\mathrm{d}x, \mathrm{d}u) = \mathrm{d}u \ \mathbb{P}_{obs}(\mathrm{d}x),\\ &\pi_1 \text{ is supported on } \mathcal{Y} \times [0,1] \times \mathcal{X}. \end{split} \end{equation} → `treatedOptimization` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Basic.lean:510`)
- definition of the paper: Let the unique positive values of the propensity score be ordered as \[ \{p(z) : z \in \mathcal{Z},\ p(z)>0\} = \{p_1, \dots, p_K\}, \] with the conventions $p_0 := 0$ and $p_{K+1} := 1$, such that $0 = p_0 < p_1 < p_2 < \cdots < p_K \leqslant p_{K+1} = 1$. Any instrument value with $p(z)=0$ is represented by the boundary level $p_0$ and contributes only the zero treated measure. We allow distinct instrument values to map to the same propensity value. This partitions the unit interval of the latent variable $U$ into the $K$ nonempty identified subintervals $I_i=(p_i,p_{i+1}]$, $i=0,\dots,K-1$, and the residual interval $I_K=(p_K,1]$, which is empty when $p_K=1$. → `finiteLevels` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Helpers/Ranges.lean:62`)
- definition of the paper: \begin{align}\label{eq:y_identified_measure} \mu_{1, i}(\mathrm{d}y) = \begin{cases} \frac{\mathbb{P}_{obs}(\mathrm{d}y, W=1 \mid p(Z) = p_1 )}{p_1}, & \text{if } i=0, \\[4pt] \frac{\mathbb{P}_{obs}(\mathrm{d}y, W=1 \mid p(Z) = p_{i+1} ) - \mathbb{P}_{obs}(\mathrm{d}y, W=1 \mid p(Z) = p_{i} )}{p_{i+1} - p_i}, & \text{if } 0 < i < K. \end{cases} \end{align} → `intervalLaw` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Helpers/Ranges.lean:449`)

Let $Q_{\omega, i}(t)$ be the quantile function of the random variable $\omega(U)$ where $U \sim \text{Unif}(p_i, p_{i+1})$. → `intervalQuantiles` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Helpers/Ranges.lean:484`)

## Theorem 3.7

**Statement.** \label{thm:continuous_lower_bound} Suppose \cref{asp:structure}, \cref{asp:target_weight}, and \cref{asp:continuity_p} hold, and $X$ is almost surely constant. Let $p(\mathcal{Z}) = \bigcup_{k=1}^{K}[\underline{p}_k, \overline{p}_k]$ be the maximal-interval decomposition in (\ref{eq:range_decomposition}). For each nonempty gap $G_k$, let $Q_{Y,k}(t)$ be the quantile function of $\mu_{1,k}$ defined in (\ref{eq:gap_identified_measure}), and $Q_{\omega,k}(t)$ the quantile function of $\omega(U)$ for $U \sim \text{Unif}(G_k)$. In the displayed sums, a term indexed by an empty gap is defined to be zero. The sharp lower bound for the $W=1$ component of the target parameter is given by \begin{equation}\label{eq:continuous_lower_bound} \begin{split} \underline{\theta}_{\omega,1}\ =\ & \underbrace{\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}(\underline{p}_{k+1}-\overline{p}_k)\int_0^1 Q_{Y,k}(t)\,Q_{\omega,k}(1-t)\,\mathrm{d}t}_{\text{OT Bound on the Gaps}} + \underbrace{\sum_{k=1}^{K}\int_{\underline{p}_k}^{\overline{p}_k}\frac{\diff}{\diff u}g_1^{\mathrm c}(u)\,\omega(u)\,\mathrm{d}u}_{\text{Point-Identified LIV on the Intervals}} \\ & \quad\quad + \underbrace{\int_{\overline{p}_K}^{1}\Big(y_{\min}\max\{0,\omega(u)\} + y_{\max}\min\{0,\omega(u)\}\Big)\mathrm{d}u}_{\text{Trivial Lower Bound on the Tail}}. \end{split} \end{equation} Similarly, the sharp upper bound is given by replacing $Q_{\omega,k}(1-t)$ with $Q_{\omega,k}(t)$ in the OT term and swapping $y_{\min}$ and $y_{\max}$ in the trivial tail term.

## Theorem 3.8

**Statement.** \label{thm:covariate_continuous_lower_bound} Suppose \cref{asp:structure}, \cref{asp:target_weight}, and \cref{asp:continuity_p} hold. For each nonempty conditional gap $G_k(x)$, let $Q_{Y, k \mid x}(t)$ be the quantile function of $\mu_{1,k\mid x}$ defined in (\ref{eq:gap_identified_measure_cov}), and $Q_{\omega, k \mid x}(t)$ the quantile function of $\omega(x, U)$ for $U \sim \text{Unif}(G_k(x))$. In the displayed sums, a term indexed by an empty conditional gap is defined to be zero. The sharp lower bound for the $W=1$ component of the target parameter is \begin{equation} \label{eq:covariate_continuous_lower_bound} \begin{split} \underline{\theta}_{\omega,1} = \mathbb{E}_{X} \Bigg[ & \sum_{k=0}^{K_X-1}(\underline{p}_{k+1}(X)-\overline{p}_k(X))\int_0^1 Q_{Y,k\mid X}(t)\,Q_{\omega,k\mid X}(1-t)\,\mathrm{d}t \\ & + \sum_{k=1}^{K_X}\int_{\underline{p}_k(X)}^{\overline{p}_k(X)} \frac{\diff}{\diff u}g_1^{\mathrm c}(u,X)\, \omega(X, u) \, \mathrm{d}u \\ & + \int_{\overline{p}_{K_X}(X)}^1 \Big( y_{\min} \max\{0, \omega(X, u)\} + y_{\max} \min\{0, \omega(X, u)\} \Big) \mathrm{d}u \Bigg]. \end{split} \end{equation} Similarly, the sharp upper bound is given by replacing $Q_{\omega,k\mid X}(1-t)$ with $Q_{\omega,k\mid X}(t)$ in the OT term and swapping $y_{\min}$ and $y_{\max}$ in the trivial tail term.

**Lean.** `Papers.TbsPrteOptimalTransport.conditional_sharp_bounds` — `lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/TCovariateLowerBound.lean:30`

Because the feasible sets for $\pi_0$ and $\pi_1$ are Cartesian products, the sharp identified interval for the full target is \[ [\underline{\theta}_\omega, \overline{\theta}_\omega] = [\underline{\theta}_{\omega,1} - \overline{\theta}_{\omega,0},\, \overline{\theta}_{\omega,1} - \underline{\theta}_{\omega,0}]. \] → `componentBounds` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Basic.lean:519`)
- definition of the paper: \begin{equation}\label{eq:ot_formulation_1} \begin{split} \min_{ \pi_1}\quad & \mathbb{E}_{\pi_1}[Y(1)\omega(X,U)]\\ \text{subject to} \quad & \int_0^{p(z,x)} \pi_1(\mathrm{d}y \mid u, x) \mathrm{d}u = H_{1,x}(p(z,x),\mathrm{d}y),\quad \mathbb P_{Z,X}\text{-a.e. }(z,x)\\ & \pi_1(\mathrm{d}x, \mathrm{d}u) = \mathrm{d}u \ \mathbb{P}_{obs}(\mathrm{d}x),\\ &\pi_1 \text{ is supported on } \mathcal{Y} \times [0,1] \times \mathcal{X}. \end{split} \end{equation} → `treatedOptimization` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Basic.lean:510`)
- definition of the paper: \begin{equation}\label{eq:canonical_submeasures} H_{w,x}(p,\mathrm{d}y)\coloneqq \mathbb P_{\mathrm{obs}}(Y\in\mathrm{d}y,W=w\mid P=p,X=x), \qquad w\in\{0,1\}. \end{equation} → `obsSubmeasure` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Basic.lean:285`)
- definition of the paper: To select canonical versions at every point of the conditional propensity support, let $K_{w,x}(\mathrm{d}y\mid u)$ be a regular conditional law of $Y(w)$ given $(U,X)=(u,x)$. Under \cref{asp:structure}, the preceding derivation gives \[ H_{w,x}(p,\mathrm{d}y)=\int_{\mathcal{R}_w(p)} K_{w,x}(\mathrm{d}y\mid u)\,\mathrm{d}u, \qquad w\in\{0,1\}, \] for $P\mid X=x$-almost every $p$. The right-hand sides are Lipschitz in total variation and hence extend the observed equivalence classes in~(\ref{eq:canonical_submeasures}) continuously to $\operatorname{supp}(P\mid X=x)$. This extension is unique: two continuous versions that agree almost surely under the conditional law of $P$ agree everywhere on its support. We henceforth use $H_{w,x}$ for these canonical observed versions. Thus $H_{w,x}$ is identified from $\mathbb P_{\mathrm{obs}}$ on the conditional propensity support, including its endpoints even when the corresponding singleton events have probability zero; the latent kernels $K_{w,x}$ themselves need not be identified. With no covariates we write $H_w$. → `canonSubmeasure` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Basic.lean:309`)
- definition of the paper: \begin{equation}\label{eq:range_decomposition_cov} p(\mathcal{Z}, x) \;=\; \bigcup_{k=1}^{K_x}[\underline{p}_k(x), \overline{p}_k(x)], \qquad 0 \leqslant \underline{p}_1(x) \leqslant \overline{p}_1(x) < \cdots < \underline{p}_{K_x}(x) \leqslant \overline{p}_{K_x}(x) \leqslant 1, \end{equation} with degenerate intervals $\underline{p}_k(x) = \overline{p}_k(x)$ corresponding to isolated conditional propensity values and boundary conventions $\overline{p}_0(x) := 0$ and $\underline{p}_{K_x+1}(x) := 1$. For each $x$, this partitions $[0,1]$ into the conditional LIV intervals $L_k(x) := [\underline{p}_k(x), \overline{p}_k(x)]$, the OT gaps $G_k(x) := (\overline{p}_k(x), \underline{p}_{k+1}(x))$, and the trivial tail $(\overline{p}_{K_x}(x), 1]$. → `CondRangeDecomposition` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Helpers/Ranges.lean:189`)
- definition of the paper: \begin{equation}\label{eq:gap_identified_measure_cov} \mu_{1,k\mid x}(\mathrm{d}y) \;:=\; \frac{H_{1,x}(\underline{p}_{k+1}(x),\mathrm{d}y)-H_{1,x}(\overline{p}_k(x),\mathrm{d}y)}{\underline{p}_{k+1}(x)-\overline{p}_k(x)}, \end{equation} for indices $k$ such that $\overline p_k(x)<\underline p_{k+1}(x)$; $H_{1,x}(0,\cdot)=0$ by definition. Empty conditional gaps are omitted and contribute zero. → `gapLaw` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Helpers/Ranges.lean:225`)
- definition of the paper: Finally, let $g_1^{\mathrm c}(p,x)\coloneqq\int_{\mathcal Y}y\,H_{1,x}(p,\mathrm{d}y)$. This canonical version is absolutely continuous in $p$ on each non-degenerate LIV interval $L_k(x)$, and every derivative below means its intervalwise Lebesgue-a.e. derivative. → `g1Canon` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Helpers/Ranges.lean:246`)
- condition of the paper: we treat the outcome domain and hence its feasible endpoints as known. → `OutcomeEndpointsKnown` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Basic.lean:111`)
- definition of the paper: For each nonempty conditional gap $G_k(x)$, let $Q_{Y, k \mid x}(t)$ be the quantile function of $\mu_{1,k\mid x}$ defined in (\ref{eq:gap_identified_measure_cov}), and $Q_{\omega, k \mid x}(t)$ the quantile function of $\omega(x, U)$ for $U \sim \text{Unif}(G_k(x))$. In the displayed sums, a term indexed by an empty conditional gap is defined to be zero. → `gapQuantiles` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Helpers/Ranges.lean:287`)
- definition of the paper: On a nonempty gap $G_k(x)$, define \begin{align*} F_{\omega,k\mid x}(v) &:=\nu_{k\mid x}\{u:\omega(x,u)\leqslant v\} \\ R_{k,x}(u,s) &:=F_{\omega,k\mid x}(\omega(x,u)-) \\ &\quad+s\Bigl[F_{\omega,k\mid x}(\omega(x,u))-F_{\omega,k\mid x}(\omega(x,u)-)\Bigr]. \end{align*} For independent $U\sim\nu_{k\mid x}$ and $S\sim\mathrm{Unif}(0,1)$, the randomized probability integral transform gives $R_{k,x}(U,S)\sim\mathrm{Unif}(0,1)$ and $\omega(x,U)=Q_{\omega,k\mid x}(R_{k,x}(U,S))$ almost surely. Set, for $u\in G_k(x)$, \begin{equation}\label{eq:pf_cts_rank_kernel} \pi_{1,x}^\star(A\mid u) :=\int_0^1\indicator\!\left\{Q_{Y,k\mid x}\bigl(1-R_{k,x}(u,s)\bigr)\in A\right\}\,\mathrm ds, \qquad A\in\mathcal B(\mathcal Y). \end{equation} → `attainingKernel` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Helpers/AttainingVersion.lean:330`)

## Proposition 4.3

**Statement.** \label{prop:discrete_reduction} Suppose \cref{asp:structure} and part~\textup{(i)} of \cref{asp:prop_gap} hold and $\mathcal{Z}$ is finite. Then the sharp lower bound in \cref{thm:covariate_continuous_lower_bound} admits the representation \begin{align}\label{eq:target_discrete_iv} \underline{\theta}_{\omega,1} = \mathbb{E}\Bigg[\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\bigg(\gamma_{\text{full},k}(X)\, J_{\text{full},k}(X) + \sum_{j=1}^{l_k} \gamma_{j,k}(X)\, J_{j-1,k}(X)\bigg) + \Delta_K(X) \Bigg]. \end{align} The sharp upper bound admits an analogous representation, obtained by replacing $Q_{Y,k\mid x}(u)$ by $Q_{Y,k\mid x}(1-u)$ inside $J_{j,k}$ and swapping $y_{\min}$ with $y_{\max}$ in $\Delta_K$.

- condition of the paper: Throughout, we assume $Y(w) \in \mathcal{Y}$ almost surely. → `PotentialSupport` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Basic.lean:117`)
- condition of the paper: \item (Consistency) $Y = WY(1) + (1-W)Y(0)$. → `Consistency` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Basic.lean:124`)
- condition of the paper: \item (Conditional Instrumental Exogeneity) $Z \indep (U,Y(0),Y(1)) \mid X$, where $\indep$ denotes statistical independence. → `InstrumentExogeneity` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Basic.lean:131`)
- condition of the paper: \item (Threshold Crossing) The treatment is selected by \begin{align}\label{eq:thresh_xing} W = \indicator (U\leqslant p(Z,X)), \end{align} where $p(Z,X) = \mathbb{P}(W=1 \mid Z, X)$. → `ThresholdCrossing` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Basic.lean:142`)
- condition of the paper: Moreover, $U \mid X \sim \mathrm{Unif}(0,1)$. → `UniformResistance` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Basic.lean:154`)
- condition of the paper: \item (Common Instrument Support) The conditional support of $Z$ given $X$ does not depend on $X$: $\mathrm{Supp}(Z \mid X=x) = \mathcal{Z}$ for all $x \in \mathcal{X}$. → `CommonInstrumentSupport` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Basic.lean:161`)
- condition of the paper: \item There exists a constant $c_{\text{gap}} > 0$ such that, for all $x \in \mathcal{X}$ and all $v,v' \in R_x = \{p(z,x),q(z,x)\}_{z \in \mathcal{Z}}$, either $v'=v$ or $|v-v'|>c_{\text{gap}}$. → `PropensityGapSeparation` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Helpers/DiscreteIV.lean:437`)
- condition of the paper: Moreover, the equality classes and their strict ordering are invariant in $x$: the sets $S_k$, $T_{j,k}$ and their indices $K,l_k$ do not depend on $x$. → `LevelOrderInvariant` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Helpers/DiscreteIV.lean:92`)
- condition of the paper: $\mathcal{Z}$ is finite. → `InstrumentFiniteReduction` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Helpers/DiscreteIV.lean:118`)
- definition of the paper: We model the alternative policy as $q(z,x) = \phi(z,x,p(z,x))$, where the known map $\phi:\mathcal Z\times\mathcal X\times[0,1]\to[0,1]$ is jointly measurable. → `policyProp` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Basic.lean:672`)
- definition of the paper: Thus, our target weight function simplifies to \begin{align*} \omega(x,u) \equiv \mathbb{E}_{\text{obs}}[\indicator(u \leqslant q(Z,X)) - \indicator(u \leqslant p(Z,X)) \mid X=x]. \end{align*} → `policyWeight` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Basic.lean:701`)
- definition of the paper: Define $q_{j,i}(x)$ as the $j$-th smallest value of the alternative policy propensity $q(z,x)$ that falls in the baseline bin $[p_i(x), p_{i+1}(x))$ for $i<K$ and in the closed final bin $[p_K(x),1]$ for $i=K$, ordered as follows: \begin{equation*} p_i(x) \leqslant q_{1,i}(x) < \cdots < q_{l_i, i}(x) < p_{i+1}(x)\quad(i<K), \end{equation*} with the boundary conventions $q_{0,i}(x) = p_i(x)$ and $q_{l_i+1,i}(x) = p_{i+1}(x)$. Let the baseline and alternative instrument level sets be \begin{align*} S_k = \{z \in \mathcal{Z} : p(z,x) = p_k(x)\}, \quad T_{j,k} = \{z \in \mathcal{Z} : q(z,x) = q_{j,k}(x)\}, \quad j=1,\ldots,l_k. \end{align*} For the final bin, the last displayed strict inequality is replaced by $q_{l_K,K}(x)\leqslant p_{K+1}(x)=1$. Here $q_{0,i}$ and $q_{l_i+1,i}$ are integration boundaries; set $T_{0,i}=T_{l_i+1,i}=\emptyset$. Thus, if $q_{1,i}=p_i$, instruments attaining that value belong only to $T_{1,i}$, while an alternative propensity equal to one belongs to $T_{l_K,K}$ and is retained in the tail term. Values of $u$ exactly on a boundary are Lebesgue-null and do not affect the target integral. → `LevelStructure` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Helpers/DiscreteIV.lean:55`)
- definition of the paper: The first two are the aggregate weights that the step weight $\omega$ assigns to instruments, \begin{align*} \gamma_{\text{full},k}(x) &\coloneqq \mathbb{P}_{\text{obs}}\!\left(Z \in \bigcup_{i=k+1}^K \bigcup_{l=0}^{l_i} T_{l,i} \mathrel{\Big|} X=x\right) - \mathbb{P}_{\text{obs}}\!\left(Z \in \bigcup_{i=k+1}^K S_i \mathrel{\Big|} X=x\right), \\ \gamma_{j,k}(x) &\coloneqq \mathbb{P}_{\text{obs}}\!\left(Z \in \bigcup_{l=j}^{l_k} T_{l,k} \mathrel{\Big|} X=x\right), \end{align*} where $\gamma_{\mathrm{full},k}\in[-1,1]$ because it differences two conditional probabilities. The next two carry the outcome distribution: with the relative threshold $\kappa_{j,k}(x) \coloneqq (q_{j,k}(x) - p_k(x))/(p_{k+1}(x) - p_k(x)) \in [0,1]$, \begin{align*} J_{\text{full},k}(x) &\coloneqq (p_{k+1}(x)-p_k(x))\,\mathbb{E}_{\mu_{1,k\mid x}}[Y], \\ J_{j,k}(x) &\coloneqq (p_{k+1}(x)-p_k(x))\int_{\kappa_{j,k}(x)}^{\kappa_{j+1,k}(x)} Q_{Y,k\mid x}(u)\,\mathrm{d}u, \end{align*} where $\mu_{1,k\mid x}$ is the identified conditional complier distribution of (\ref{eq:gap_identified_measure_cov}) and $Q_{Y,k\mid x}$ is its quantile function. The fifth is the trivial-bound boundary term \begin{equation*} \Delta_K(x) \coloneqq y_{\min}\, \mathbb{E}_{\text{obs}}\!\left[ \indicator\!\Big(Z \in \bigcup_{j=1}^{l_K} T_{j,K}\Big) (q(Z,X) - p_K(X)) \mathrel{\Big|} X=x \right]. \end{equation*} → `discreteComponents` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Helpers/DiscreteIV.lean:229`)
- definition of the paper: \begin{equation}\label{eq:gap_identified_measure_cov} \mu_{1,k\mid x}(\mathrm{d}y) \;:=\; \frac{H_{1,x}(\underline{p}_{k+1}(x),\mathrm{d}y)-H_{1,x}(\overline{p}_k(x),\mathrm{d}y)}{\underline{p}_{k+1}(x)-\overline{p}_k(x)}, \end{equation} for indices $k$ such that $\overline p_k(x)<\underline p_{k+1}(x)$; $H_{1,x}(0,\cdot)=0$ by definition. Empty conditional gaps are omitted and contribute zero. → `gapLaw` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Helpers/Ranges.lean:225`)
- definition of the paper: For each nonempty conditional gap $G_k(x)$, let $Q_{Y, k \mid x}(t)$ be the quantile function of $\mu_{1,k\mid x}$ defined in (\ref{eq:gap_identified_measure_cov}), and $Q_{\omega, k \mid x}(t)$ the quantile function of $\omega(x, U)$ for $U \sim \text{Unif}(G_k(x))$. In the displayed sums, a term indexed by an empty conditional gap is defined to be zero. → `gapQuantiles` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Helpers/Ranges.lean:287`)

## Proposition 4.4

**Statement.** \label{prop:J_specialization} Fix $k \in \{0,\dots,K-1\}$. Set the lower boundary $\nu_{0,k}(x)\equiv y_{\min}$, and let $\nu_{j,k}(x) \coloneqq Q_{Y,k\mid x}(\kappa_{j,k}(x))$ for $j \in \{1,\dots,l_k\}$. \begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)] \item \textbf{Continuous outcome.} If $\mu_{1,k\mid X}$ is continuous at $\nu_{j,k}(X)$ and $\nu_{j+1,k}(X)$ almost surely, then, for $j \in \{0,\dots,l_k-1\}$, \begin{equation}\label{eq:J_cexpectation} J_{j,k}(X) = (p_{k+1}(X) - p_k(X))\,\mathbb{E}_{\mu_{1,k\mid X}}\!\big[ Y\,\indicator\!\big(\nu_{j,k}(X) < Y \leqslant \nu_{j+1,k}(X)\big) \big]. \end{equation} \item \textbf{Binary outcome.} If $Y \in \{0,1\}$, then $J_{\text{full},k}(X) = P_{1,k+1}(X) - P_{1,k}(X)$ with $P_{1,k}(x) \coloneqq \mathbb{E}_{\text{obs}}[YW \mid Z \in S_k, X=x]$ for $k\geqslant1$ and the boundary convention $P_{1,0}(x)\equiv0$, and for $j \in \{1,\dots,l_k\}$, \begin{equation}\label{eq:h_jk_binary} J_{j-1,k}(X) = h_{j,k}^{-}(X) \coloneqq \max\!\Big(0,\; q_{j,k}(X) - \max\!\big(q_{j-1,k}(X),\; p_{k+1}(X) - J_{\text{full},k}(X)\big)\Big). \end{equation} Consequently, (\ref{eq:target_discrete_iv}) collapses to \begin{align}\label{eq:binary_lower_bound} \underline{\theta}_{\omega,1} = \mathbb{E}\Bigg[\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\bigg(\gamma_{\text{full},k}(X)\, J_{\text{full},k}(X) + \sum_{j=1}^{l_k} \gamma_{j,k}(X)\, h_{j,k}^{-}(X)\bigg) + \Delta_K(X)\Bigg]. \end{align} \end{enumerate}

**Lean.** `Papers.TbsPrteOptimalTransport.J_specialization` — `lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/TJSpecialization.lean:117`

**Status.** proved in Lean, under 18 condition(s) made explicit. **Axioms.** the standard ones only (propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound).

The next two carry the outcome distribution: with the relative threshold $\kappa_{j,k}(x) \coloneqq (q_{j,k}(x) - p_k(x))/(p_{k+1}(x) - p_k(x)) \in [0,1]$, \begin{align*} J_{\text{full},k}(x) &\coloneqq (p_{k+1}(x)-p_k(x))\,\mathbb{E}_{\mu_{1,k\mid x}}[Y], \\ J_{j,k}(x) &\coloneqq (p_{k+1}(x)-p_k(x))\int_{\kappa_{j,k}(x)}^{\kappa_{j+1,k}(x)} Q_{Y,k\mid x}(u)\,\mathrm{d}u, \end{align*} where $\mu_{1,k\mid x}$ is the identified conditional complier distribution of (\ref{eq:gap_identified_measure_cov}) and $Q_{Y,k\mid x}$ is its quantile function. The fifth is the trivial-bound boundary term \begin{equation*} \Delta_K(x) \coloneqq y_{\min}\, \mathbb{E}_{\text{obs}}\!\left[ \indicator\!\Big(Z \in \bigcup_{j=1}^{l_K} T_{j,K}\Big) (q(Z,X) - p_K(X)) \mathrel{\Big|} X=x \right]. \end{equation*} → `discreteComponents` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Helpers/DiscreteIV.lean:229`)
- definition of the paper: Define $q_{j,i}(x)$ as the $j$-th smallest value of the alternative policy propensity $q(z,x)$ that falls in the baseline bin $[p_i(x), p_{i+1}(x))$ for $i<K$ and in the closed final bin $[p_K(x),1]$ for $i=K$, ordered as follows: \begin{equation*} p_i(x) \leqslant q_{1,i}(x) < \cdots < q_{l_i, i}(x) < p_{i+1}(x)\quad(i<K), \end{equation*} with the boundary conventions $q_{0,i}(x) = p_i(x)$ and $q_{l_i+1,i}(x) = p_{i+1}(x)$. Let the baseline and alternative instrument level sets be \begin{align*} S_k = \{z \in \mathcal{Z} : p(z,x) = p_k(x)\}, \quad T_{j,k} = \{z \in \mathcal{Z} : q(z,x) = q_{j,k}(x)\}, \quad j=1,\ldots,l_k. \end{align*} For the final bin, the last displayed strict inequality is replaced by $q_{l_K,K}(x)\leqslant p_{K+1}(x)=1$. Here $q_{0,i}$ and $q_{l_i+1,i}$ are integration boundaries; set $T_{0,i}=T_{l_i+1,i}=\emptyset$. Thus, if $q_{1,i}=p_i$, instruments attaining that value belong only to $T_{1,i}$, while an alternative propensity equal to one belongs to $T_{l_K,K}$ and is retained in the tail term. Values of $u$ exactly on a boundary are Lebesgue-null and do not affect the target integral. → `LevelStructure` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Helpers/DiscreteIV.lean:55`)
- definition of the paper: Set the lower boundary $\nu_{0,k}(x)\equiv y_{\min}$, and let $\nu_{j,k}(x) \coloneqq Q_{Y,k\mid x}(\kappa_{j,k}(x))$ for $j \in \{1,\dots,l_k\}$. → `bandBoundary` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Helpers/DiscreteIV.lean:282`)
- definition of the paper: \begin{equation}\label{eq:gap_identified_measure_cov} \mu_{1,k\mid x}(\mathrm{d}y) \;:=\; \frac{H_{1,x}(\underline{p}_{k+1}(x),\mathrm{d}y)-H_{1,x}(\overline{p}_k(x),\mathrm{d}y)}{\underline{p}_{k+1}(x)-\overline{p}_k(x)}, \end{equation} for indices $k$ such that $\overline p_k(x)<\underline p_{k+1}(x)$; $H_{1,x}(0,\cdot)=0$ by definition. Empty conditional gaps are omitted and contribute zero. → `gapLaw` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Helpers/Ranges.lean:225`)
- condition of the paper: If $\mu_{1,k\mid X}$ is continuous at $\nu_{j,k}(X)$ and $\nu_{j+1,k}(X)$ almost surely, → `GapLawContinuousAtBoundaries` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Helpers/DiscreteIV.lean:290`)
- condition of the paper: If $Y \in \{0,1\}$, → `OutcomeBinary` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Helpers/DiscreteIV.lean:296`)

## Theorem 4.7

**Statement.** \label{thm:dml_normality} Suppose \cref{asp:structure}, \cref{asp:phi_smooth}, \cref{asp:prop_gap}, and \cref{asp:overlap} hold and $\mathcal{Z}$ is finite. Suppose the pre-recovery first stage fitted on $I_1$ satisfies $\max_{z\in\mathcal Z}\|\hat p(z,\cdot)-p(z,\cdot)\|_{L_2}=o_P(n^{-1/4})$. Given the recovered level sets, suppose the remaining score nuisances fitted on $I_1$ are clipped to their known bounded ranges, every estimated conditional probability is clipped to $[0,1]$ before any difference is formed, $\hat\pi_k$ and $\hat\tau_{j,k}$ are clipped further to $[c_\pi/2,1]$, and the estimators converge componentwise at rate $o_P(n^{-1/4})$ in $L_2$. Suppose in addition that one of the following holds. \begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}] \item \textbf{Continuous outcome.} The nuisances include the boundary quantiles $\nu_{j,k}$, and, uniformly in $X$, each relevant conditional law $Y\mid X,Z\in S_{k'},W=1$ has a density bounded in a fixed neighborhood of every true boundary $\nu_{j,k}(X)$, $j=1,\ldots,l_k$, for $k'=k+1$ and also for $k'=k$ when $k\geqslant1$; at the fixed boundary $\nu_{0,k}=y_{\min}$, these conditional laws have no atom at $y_{\min}$. \item \textbf{Discrete outcome.} The outcome is binary, \cref{asp:discrete_outcome_gap} holds, and the nuisances are $p_k$, $P_{1,k}$, $\pi_k$, $\tau_{j,k}$, $a_{j,k}$, $\gamma_{\mathrm{full},k}$, $\gamma_{j,k}$, and $\gamma_K$. \end{enumerate} Then the sample-split estimator (\ref{eq:estimator_dml}) is $\sqrt{n}$-consistent and asymptotically normal: \begin{equation*} \sqrt{n/2} \big( \hat{\underline{\theta}}_{\omega, 1} - \underline{\theta}_{\omega, 1} \big) \xrightarrow{d} \mathcal{N}(0, \sigma^2), \end{equation*} where the asymptotic variance is the variance of the true orthogonal score, $\sigma^2 = \mathbb{E}\big[ ({\psi}(O; \eta) - \underline{\theta}_{\omega, 1})^2 \big]$. It can be consistently estimated by its sample analog $\hat{\sigma}^2 = \frac{1}{|I_2|} \sum_{i \in I_2} \big(\hat{\psi}(O_i; \hat{\eta}) - \hat{\underline{\theta}}_{\omega, 1}\big)^2$.

**Lean.** `Papers.TbsPrteOptimalTransport.dml_asymptotic_normality` — `lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/TDmlNormality.lean:485`

**Status.** proved in Lean, under 35 condition(s) made explicit. **Axioms.** the standard ones only (propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound).

- reading: $Y=WY(1)+(1-W)Y(0)$ almost surely under the structural population law. → `ConsistencyAEReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Basic.lean:177`)
- reading: $W=\indicator(U\leqslant p(Z,X))$ almost surely under the structural population law. → `ThresholdCrossingAEReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Basic.lean:182`)
- reading: $\mathcal L(U\mid X=x)=\mathrm{Unif}(0,1)$ for $\mathbb P_X$-almost every $x$. → `UniformResistanceAEReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Basic.lean:187`)
- reading: Any two structural laws satisfying Structural Assumptions and inducing the same $\mathbb P_{obs}$ give the same $\omega(x,u)$ for $\mathbb P_{obs}(\mathrm dx)\,\mathrm du$-almost every $(x,u)$. → `WeightIdentifiableAEReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Basic.lean:229`)
- reading: The continuity condition holds for $\mathbb P_X$-almost every $x\in\mathcal X$. → `PropensityContinuityAEReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Helpers/Ranges.lean:42`)
- reading: For every $z\in\mathcal Z$ and $x\in\mathcal X$, $p\mapsto\phi(z,x,p)$ is differentiable on $(0,1)$ and has finite right and left derivatives at $p=0$ and $p=1$, respectively; these are its endpoint values of $\partial\phi/\partial p$. → `PolicyDifferentiableEndpointReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Basic.lean:726`)
- reading: There exist finite constants $C,L\geqslant0$ such that, for all $z\in\mathcal Z$, $x\in\mathcal X$, and $p,p'\in[0,1]$, $|\partial\phi/\partial p(z,x,p)|\leqslant C$ and $|\partial\phi/\partial p(z,x,p)-\partial\phi/\partial p(z,x,p')|\leqslant L|p-p'|$. → `PolicyDerivativeConstantsReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Basic.lean:732`)
- reading: For every $x\in\mathcal X$ and every nonempty $T_{j,k}$, $\tau_{j,k}(x)\geqslant c_\pi$, where $c_\pi>0$ is the same constant used for $\pi_k$ in \cref{asp:overlap}. → `PolicyOverlapSameConstantReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Helpers/DiscreteIV.lean:266`)
- reading: For $\rho\in\{\mu,\nu\}$ and $t\in(0,1)$, $Q_\rho(t)=\inf\{a\in\mathbb R:\rho((\! -\infty,a])\geqslant t\}$. → `LowerQuantileReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Helpers/Transport.lean:69`)
- reading: For each fixed $k\in\{0,\dots,K-1\}$ and $j\in\{0,\dots,l_k-1\}$ to which part~(i) is applied, its continuity hypothesis is $\mu_{1,k\mid X}(\{\nu_{j,k}(X)\})=\mu_{1,k\mid X}(\{\nu_{j+1,k}(X)\})=0$ almost surely under the marginal law of $X$. → `BoundaryContinuityNoAtomReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Helpers/DiscreteIV.lean:302`)
- reading: For a fresh $X$ with the population marginal law and independent of $I_1$, $\|\hat p(z,\cdot)-p(z,\cdot)\|_{L_2}^2=\mathbb E[(\hat p(z,X)-p(z,X))^2\mid I_1]$, and $n^{1/4}\max_{z\in\mathcal Z}\|\hat p(z,\cdot)-p(z,\cdot)\|_{L_2}\xrightarrow{P}0$ as $n\to\infty$. → `FirstStageRateFreshDrawReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Helpers/DMLProcedureCore.lean:365`)
- reading: Pointwise in $x$, the fitted probability, propensity, share, and indicator-mean nuisances lie in $[0,1]$, $\hat\gamma_{\mathrm{full},k}(x)\in[-1,1]$, $\hat\nu_{j,k}(x),\hat J_{\mathrm{full},k}^{\pm}(x)\in[y_{\min},y_{\max}]$, and $\hat J_{j,k}^{\pm}(x)\in[\min\{0,y_{\min}\},\max\{0,y_{\max}\}]$, for each applicable fitted component; denominator clipping is applied further as stated. → `ClippingRangesReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Helpers/DMLProcedureCore.lean:548`)
- reading: Componentwise $L_2$ errors integrate over the marginal law of $X$ under $\mathbb P_{\mathrm{obs}}$, holding each fitted function and any instrument argument $z$ fixed; $o_P(n^{-1/4})$ refers to convergence in probability over the observed sample and random partition as $n\to\infty$. → `NuisanceRatesComponentwiseReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Helpers/DMLProcedureCore.lean:375`)
- reading: $Y\in\{0,1\}$ almost surely. → `OutcomeBinaryAEReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Helpers/DMLProcedureCore.lean:468`)
- reading: Every constraint in $\eqref{eq:ot_formulation}$ is imposed simultaneously for both $w\in\{0,1\}$. → `ConstraintsBothStatesReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Basic.lean:453`)
- reading: Two remaining labeled vertices are connected exactly when their averaged values differ by less than $c_{\mathrm{gap}}/2$. → `RecoveryEdgeStrict` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Helpers/DMLProcedureCore.lean:186`)
- reading: For $\mathbb P_X$-almost every $x$, the intervals $L_k(x)$, $1\leqslant k\leqslant K_x$, the gaps $G_k(x)$, $0\leqslant k<K_x$, and the tail $(\overline p_{K_x}(x),1]$ partition $[0,1]$ up to Lebesgue-null endpoints. → `CondRegionsPartitionNullReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Helpers/Ranges.lean:210`)
- reading: The identified outcome components $J_{\text{full},k}$ and $J_{j,k}$ are defined for $0\leqslant k<K$, with $0\leqslant j\leqslant l_k$ for $J_{j,k}$; the final interval is represented by $\Delta_K$, without an identified law $\mu_{1,K\mid x}$. → `ComponentIndexRangeReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Helpers/DiscreteIV.lean:310`)
- reading: For $0<t<1$, use the standard lower generalized quantile $Q_{Y,k\mid x}(t)=\inf\{y\in\mathbb R:\mu_{1,k\mid x}(\mathcal Y\cap(-\infty,y])\geqslant t\}$. → `LevelGapQuantileLowerInverse` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Helpers/DiscreteIV.lean:332`)
- reading: For $j=1,\ldots,l_k$, interpret $\nu_{j,k}(x)=Q_{Y,k\mid x}(\kappa_{j,k}(x))$ using $Q_{Y,k\mid x}(t)=\inf\{y\in\mathbb R:\mu_{1,k\mid x}(\mathcal Y\cap(-\infty,y])\geqslant t\}$ for $0<t<1$, with $\nu_{0,k}(x)=y_{\min}$. → `BandBoundaryReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Helpers/DiscreteIV.lean:341`)
- reading: In this definition, $\psi_{\mathrm{full},k}^{\mathrm{prod}}$ and $\psi_{j,k}^{\mathrm{prod}}$ are the binary-outcome components of Section~\ref{app:scores_discrete}, and $\psi_{\Delta_K}$ is the common tail component of Section~\ref{app:scores_continuous}. → `BinaryScoreComponentsReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Helpers/ScoresBinary.lean:224`)
- reading: The binary nuisance list supplements the separately fitted first-stage propensity, so $\eta(x)=\{p(z,x),p_k(x),P_{1,k}(x),\pi_k(x),\tau_{j,k}(x),a_{j,k}(z,x),\gamma_{\mathrm{full},k}(x),\gamma_{j,k}(x),\gamma_K(x)\}_{j,k}$, with $p_0=P_{1,0}=0$ fixed and no fitted $\pi_0$. → `BinaryNuisanceListReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Helpers/ScoresBinary.lean:234`)
- reading: The structural-zero assignment in Algorithm~\ref{alg:dml_continuous_outcome} is $\widehat S_0=\{z:\bar p_2(z)\leqslant\rho_n\}$, with deterministic $\rho_n\downarrow0$ satisfying $n^{1/4}\rho_n\to\infty$. → `StructuralZeroThreshold` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Helpers/DMLProcedureCore.lean:211`)
- reading: The estimated baseline bins used to index $\widehat T_{j,k}$ include their left endpoints and exclude their right endpoints for $k<K$; the final bin for $k=K$ includes both endpoints, including $1$. → `RecoveredBinConventionReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Helpers/DMLProcedureCore.lean:225`)
- reading: For $0<t<1$, use the lower generalized inverses $Q_{Y,k\mid x}(t)=\inf\{y\in\mathbb R:\mu_{1,k\mid x}(\mathcal Y\cap(-\infty,y])\geqslant t\}$ and $Q_{\omega,k\mid x}(t)=\inf\{v\in\mathbb R:F_{\omega,k\mid x}(v)\geqslant t\}$. → `CondGapQuantilesLowerInverse` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Helpers/Ranges.lean:298`)
- reading: At each evaluation covariate $X_i$, $(\hat P^*_{1,1}(X_i),\ldots,\hat P^*_{1,K}(X_i))$ is the nondecreasing sort of the clipped vector $(\hat P_{1,1}(X_i),\ldots,\hat P_{1,K}(X_i))$, with $\hat P^*_{1,0}(X_i)=0$; this same vector is used in every score plug-in and residual, without a separate gap projection. → `RearrangementSortReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Helpers/DMLProcedureCore.lean:482`)
- reading: Interpret the kernel formula with any fixed jointly measurable $\mathcal Y$-valued extension of $Q_{Y,k\mid x}$ to ranks $0$ and $1$; the asserted gap marginal and attaining objective hold for every such extension. → `RankKernelExtensionReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Helpers/Ranges.lean:339`)
- reading: In this definition, all components of $\psi(O;\eta)$ are those of Section~\ref{app:scores_continuous}, in particular $\psi_{j,k}^{\mathrm{prod}}=\gamma_{j,k}\psi_{j-1,k}^{J}+J_{j-1,k}(\indicator(Z\in\bigcup_{l=j}^{l_k}T_{l,k})-\gamma_{j,k})$. → `ContinuousScoreComponentsReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Helpers/Scores.lean:424`)
- reading: For every nonempty conditional gap $G_k(x)$ and $0<t<1$, $(Q_{Y,k\mid x}(t),Q_{\omega,k\mid x}(t))=(\inf\{y\in\mathbb R:\mu_{1,k\mid x}(\mathcal Y\cap(-\infty,y])\geqslant t\},\inf\{v\in\mathbb R:F_{\omega,k\mid x}(v)\geqslant t\})$. → `CondGapQuantilesLowerInverseReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Helpers/Ranges.lean:314`)
- reading: For $0<t<1$, $(Q_\mu(t),Q_\nu(t))=(\inf\{a\in\mathbb R:\mu(( -\infty,a])\geqslant t\},\inf\{b\in\mathbb R:\nu(( -\infty,b])\geqslant t\})$. → `LowerQuantileReading'` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Helpers/Transport.lean:79`)
- reading: The boundary quantile uses the bounded-domain lower generalized inverse $Q_{Y,k\mid x}(t)=\inf\{y\in\mathcal Y:\mu_{1,k\mid x}(\mathcal Y\cap(-\infty,y])\geqslant t\}$ for $0\leqslant t<1$, so $Q_{Y,k\mid x}(0)=y_{\min}$. → `BandBoundaryBoundedLowerInverse` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Helpers/DiscreteIV.lean:322`)
- reading: Every nuisance-error norm in this theorem is the population norm $\|\hat f-f\|_{L_2}^2=\mathbb E_X[|\hat f(X)-f(X)|^2]$, with the fitted function held fixed during integration. → `NuisanceL2NormPopulationReading` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Helpers/DMLProcedureCore.lean:274`)
- condition of the paper: Throughout, we assume $Y(w) \in \mathcal{Y}$ almost surely. → `PotentialSupport` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Basic.lean:117`)
- condition of the paper: \item (Consistency) $Y = WY(1) + (1-W)Y(0)$. → `Consistency` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Basic.lean:124`)
- condition of the paper: \item (Conditional Instrumental Exogeneity) $Z \indep (U,Y(0),Y(1)) \mid X$, where $\indep$ denotes statistical independence. → `InstrumentExogeneity` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Basic.lean:131`)
- condition of the paper: \item (Threshold Crossing) The treatment is selected by \begin{align}\label{eq:thresh_xing} W = \indicator (U\leqslant p(Z,X)), \end{align} where $p(Z,X) = \mathbb{P}(W=1 \mid Z, X)$. → `ThresholdCrossing` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Basic.lean:142`)
- condition of the paper: Moreover, $U \mid X \sim \mathrm{Unif}(0,1)$. → `UniformResistance` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Basic.lean:154`)
- condition of the paper: \item (Common Instrument Support) The conditional support of $Z$ given $X$ does not depend on $X$: $\mathrm{Supp}(Z \mid X=x) = \mathcal{Z}$ for all $x \in \mathcal{X}$. → `CommonInstrumentSupport` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Basic.lean:161`)
- condition of the paper: The map $\phi(z,x,p)$ is differentiable with respect to $p$, → `PolicyDifferentiable` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Basic.lean:709`)
- condition of the paper: $\frac{\partial \phi}{\partial p}$ is bounded and uniformly Lipschitz in $p$ for all $z \in \mathcal{Z}$ and $x \in \mathcal{X}$. → `PolicyDerivativeBoundedLipschitz` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Basic.lean:718`)
- condition of the paper: \item There exists a constant $c_{\text{gap}} > 0$ such that, for all $x \in \mathcal{X}$ and all $v,v' \in R_x = \{p(z,x),q(z,x)\}_{z \in \mathcal{Z}}$, either $v'=v$ or $|v-v'|>c_{\text{gap}}$. → `PropensityGapSeparation` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Helpers/DiscreteIV.lean:437`)
- condition of the paper: Moreover, the equality classes and their strict ordering are invariant in $x$: the sets $S_k$, $T_{j,k}$ and their indices $K,l_k$ do not depend on $x$. → `LevelOrderInvariant` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Helpers/DiscreteIV.lean:92`)
- condition of the paper: \item For every $k=0,\ldots,K-1$ and every nonempty $T_{j,k}$, $q_{j,k}(x)\neq p_k(x)$ for all $x$. → `PolicyLevelOffBoundary` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Helpers/DiscreteIV.lean:111`)
- condition of the paper: There exists a constant $c_\pi > 0$ such that $\pi_k(x) \geqslant c_\pi$ for every $x \in \mathcal{X}$ and observed positive-propensity level $k \in \{1, \dots, K\}$, → `BaselineOverlap` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Helpers/DiscreteIV.lean:449`)
- condition of the paper: $\tau_{j,k}(x)\geqslant c_\pi$ for every nonempty alternative-policy level set $T_{j,k}$. → `PolicyOverlap` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Helpers/DiscreteIV.lean:257`)
- condition of the paper: $\mathcal{Z}$ is finite. → `InstrumentFiniteDML` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Helpers/DMLProcedureCore.lean:99`)
- condition of the paper: Suppose the pre-recovery first stage fitted on $I_1$ satisfies $\max_{z\in\mathcal Z}\|\hat p(z,\cdot)-p(z,\cdot)\|_{L_2}=o_P(n^{-1/4})$. → `FirstStageRate` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Helpers/DMLProcedureCore.lean:305`)
- condition of the paper: Given the recovered level sets, suppose the remaining score nuisances fitted on $I_1$ are clipped to their known bounded ranges, → `ScoreNuisancesClipped` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Helpers/DMLProcedureCore.lean:542`)
- condition of the paper: every estimated conditional probability is clipped to $[0,1]$ before any difference is formed, → `ProbabilitiesClippedBeforeDifference` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Helpers/DMLProcedureCore.lean:554`)
- condition of the paper: $\hat\pi_k$ and $\hat\tau_{j,k}$ are clipped further to $[c_\pi/2,1]$, → `DenominatorsClipped` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Helpers/DMLProcedureCore.lean:562`)
- condition of the paper: the estimators converge componentwise at rate $o_P(n^{-1/4})$ in $L_2$. → `NuisanceRates` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Helpers/DMLProcedureCore.lean:314`)
- condition of the paper: The nuisances include the boundary quantiles $\nu_{j,k}$, → `ContinuousNuisanceList` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Helpers/DMLProcedureCore.lean:440`)
- condition of the paper: uniformly in $X$, each relevant conditional law $Y\mid X,Z\in S_{k'},W=1$ has a density bounded in a fixed neighborhood of every true boundary $\nu_{j,k}(X)$, $j=1,\ldots,l_k$, for $k'=k+1$ and also for $k'=k$ when $k\geqslant1$; → `BoundaryDensitiesBounded` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Helpers/DMLProcedureCore.lean:448`)
- condition of the paper: at the fixed boundary $\nu_{0,k}=y_{\min}$, these conditional laws have no atom at $y_{\min}$. → `NoAtomAtLowerEndpoint` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Helpers/DMLProcedureCore.lean:458`)
- condition of the paper: The outcome is binary → `OutcomeBinaryAE` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Helpers/DMLProcedureCore.lean:464`)
- condition of the paper: \label{asp:discrete_outcome_gap} There exists $c_h>0$ such that, for every $k=0,\ldots,K-1$ with $l_k\geqslant1$ and $j=0,\ldots,l_k$, almost surely, \[ \left|p_{k+1}(X)-J_{\mathrm{full},k}(X)-q_{j,k}(X)\right|\geqslant c_h. \] → `DiscreteOutcomeGap` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Helpers/DiscreteIV.lean:460`)
- condition of the paper: Throughout the paper, we assume that all domains are compact, carry their Borel $\sigma$-fields, and are standard Borel spaces. → `DomainsCompactBorel` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Basic.lean:102`)
- condition of the paper: we treat the outcome domain and hence its feasible endpoints as known. → `OutcomeEndpointsKnown` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Basic.lean:111`)
- definition of the paper: \begin{equation}\label{eq:estimator_dml} \hat{\underline{\theta}}_{\omega, 1} = \frac{1}{|I_2|} \sum_{i \in I_2} \Bigg[ \sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\bigg(\hat{\psi}_{\text{full},k}^{\text{prod}}(O_i; \hat{\eta}) + \sum_{j=1}^{l_k} \hat{\psi}_{j,k}^{\text{prod}}(O_i; \hat{\eta})\bigg) + \hat{\psi}_{\Delta_K}(O_i; \hat{\eta}) \Bigg], \end{equation} → `dmlEstimator` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Helpers/DMLProcedure.lean:119`)
- definition of the paper: \paragraph{Influence-function residuals.} The scaled influence-function residuals used to debias the conditional-expectation terms, evaluated at an observation $O = (X, Z, W, Y)$, are: \begin{align*} IF_{J^+}(O) &= \frac{1}{\pi_{k+1}(X)} \indicator(Z \in S_{k+1}) W \Big( Y \indicator(\nu_{j,k}(X) < Y \leqslant \nu_{j+1,k}(X)) - J_{j,k}^+(X) \Big) \\ IF_{M_{j,k}^+}(O) &= \frac{1}{\pi_{k+1}(X)} \indicator(Z \in S_{k+1}) W \Big( \indicator(Y \leqslant \nu_{j,k}(X)) - M_{j,k}^+(X) \Big), \end{align*} with $IF_{J^-}$ and $IF_{M_{j,k}^-}$ defined analogously over $S_k$ when $k\geqslant1$. When $k=0$, $p_0\equiv0$ is a structural boundary: all minus-side conditional expectations and residuals are omitted and are defined as zero solely for notational convenience. In particular, the score never divides by $\pi_0(X)$. \paragraph{Riesz representers.} Because $p_k(X)$ and $p_{k+1}(X)$ act as structural interval boundaries, the augmented functional yields non-zero derivatives with respect to these parameters. The exact Riesz representers for the marginal probabilities are: \begin{align*} \alpha_{p_k}(X) &= -J_{j,k}^-(X) - \nu_{j,k}(X)M_{j,k}^-(X) + \nu_{j+1,k}(X)M_{j+1,k}^-(X) \\ &\quad + \indicator(j\geqslant1)\nu_{j,k}(X) - \nu_{j+1,k}(X), \\ \alpha_{p_{k+1}}(X) &= J_{j,k}^+(X) + \nu_{j,k}(X)M_{j,k}^+(X) - \nu_{j+1,k}(X)M_{j+1,k}^+(X). \end{align*} The first expression applies to $k\geqslant1$; the $p_0$ correction is omitted because $p_0$ is known. The indicator is essential at the lower index: $q_{0,k}\equiv p_k$, so $q_{0,k}-p_k\equiv0$ has zero derivative with respect to $p_k$. Thus the representer for the score component actually used at $j=0$ contains no standalone $+\nu_{0,k}$ term. \paragraph{Pooled policy-level residuals.} For every nonempty alternative-policy level set, define \begin{equation*} R_{q,j,k}(O) \coloneqq \frac{1}{\tau_{j,k}(X)} \sum_{z\in T_{j,k}} \frac{\partial\phi}{\partial p}(z,X,p(z,X))\, \indicator(Z=z)\bigl(W-p(z,X)\bigr). \end{equation*} Set $R_{q,0,k}=R_{q,l_k+1,k}\equiv0$ for the structural integration boundaries. The normalization treats a tied policy value as one boundary; summing category-specific inverse-probability residuals without this normalization would count the same boundary once for every instrument in $T_{j,k}$. Multiple instruments may still share a regular policy level. Part~\textup{(ii)} of \cref{asp:prop_gap} only prevents such a nonempty policy level from coinciding with the structural left boundary $q_{0,k}=p_k$ when $k<K$. \paragraph{Score for $\mathbb E[J_{j,k}(X)]$.} Aggregating the expectation residuals and the structural parameter influences, the complete uncentered orthogonal score for the integral component $J_{j,k}(X)$ is: \begin{align*} \psi_{j,k}^J(O; \eta) &= J_{j,k}(X) + IF_{J^+}(O) - IF_{J^-}(O) \\ &\quad + \nu_{j,k}(X) \Big( p_{k+1}(X)M_{j,k}^+(X) - p_k(X)M_{j,k}^-(X) - (q_{j,k}(X) - p_k(X)) \\ &\quad \quad \quad \quad \quad \quad + IF_{M_{j,k}^+}(O) - IF_{M_{j,k}^-}(O) \Big) \\ &\quad - \nu_{j+1,k}(X) \Big( p_{k+1}(X)M_{j+1,k}^+(X) - p_k(X)M_{j+1,k}^-(X) - (q_{j+1,k}(X) - p_k(X)) \\ &\quad \quad \quad \quad \quad \quad + IF_{M_{j+1,k}^+}(O) - IF_{M_{j+1,k}^-}(O) \Big) \\ &\quad + \alpha_{p_k}(X) \frac{1}{\pi_k(X)} \indicator(Z \in S_k) \big(W - p_k(X)\big) \\ &\quad + \alpha_{p_{k+1}}(X) \frac{1}{\pi_{k+1}(X)} \indicator(Z \in S_{k+1}) \big(W - p_{k+1}(X)\big) \\ &\quad - \nu_{j,k}(X) R_{q,j,k}(O) \\ &\quad + \nu_{j+1,k}(X) R_{q,j+1,k}(O). \end{align*} \paragraph{Score for $\mathbb E[J_{\mathrm{full},k}(X)]$.} The analogous score for the full-interval component omits the quantile-constraint terms: \begin{align*} \psi_{\text{full},k}^J(O; \eta) &= J_{\text{full},k}(X) \\ &\quad + \frac{1}{\pi_{k+1}(X)} \indicator(Z \in S_{k+1}) W \Big( Y - J_{\text{full},k}^+(X) \Big) \\ &\quad - \frac{1}{\pi_k(X)} \indicator(Z \in S_k) W \Big( Y - J_{\text{full},k}^-(X) \Big) \\ &\quad + J_{\text{full},k}^+(X) \frac{1}{\pi_{k+1}(X)} \indicator(Z \in S_{k+1}) \big(W - p_{k+1}(X)\big) \\ &\quad - J_{\text{full},k}^-(X) \frac{1}{\pi_k(X)} \indicator(Z \in S_k) \big(W - p_k(X)\big). \end{align*} Every minus-side term in the two preceding score displays, including the $p_k$ boundary correction, is omitted when $k=0$, according to the structural-boundary convention above. \paragraph{Product scores.} To account for the estimation of the weighting regressions $\gamma(X)$, we construct the orthogonal product scores using the chain rule for influence functions. The uncentered product score for the fractional interval component is: \begin{align*} \psi_{j,k}^{\text{prod}}(O; \eta) &= \gamma_{j,k}(X) \psi_{j-1,k}^J(O; \eta) + J_{j-1,k}(X) \left( \indicator\Big(Z \in \bigcup_{l=j}^{l_k} T_{l,k}\Big) - \gamma_{j,k}(X) \right), \end{align*} and similarly for the full interval component: \begin{align*} \psi_{\text{full},k}^{\text{prod}}(O; \eta) &= \gamma_{\text{full},k}(X) \psi_{\text{full},k}^J(O; \eta) \\ &\quad + J_{\text{full},k}(X) \left( \indicator\Big(Z \in \bigcup_{m=k+1}^K \bigcup_{l=0}^{l_m} T_{l,m}\Big) - \indicator\Big(Z \in \bigcup_{m=k+1}^K S_m\Big) - \gamma_{\text{full},k}(X) \right). \end{align*} \paragraph{Trivial bound score.} The uncentered orthogonal score for the trivial bound component on the final interval, $\Delta_K(X)$, is: \begin{align*} \psi_{\Delta_K}(O; \eta) &= y_{\min} \indicator\Big(Z \in \bigcup_{j=1}^{l_K} T_{j,K}\Big) \big( q(Z,X) - p_K(X) \big) \\ &\quad + y_{\min} \indicator\Big(Z \in \bigcup_{j=1}^{l_K} T_{j,K}\Big) \frac{\partial \phi}{\partial p}(Z,X, p(Z,X)) \big( W - p(Z,X) \big) \\ &\quad - y_{\min} \gamma_K(X) \frac{1}{\pi_K(X)} \indicator(Z \in S_K) \big( W - p_K(X) \big). \end{align*} → `contScoreFamily` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Helpers/Scores.lean:520`)
- definition of the paper: For $k\geqslant1$, the standard IPW residuals for $P_{1,k}(X)$ and $p_k(X)$ are: \begin{align*} R_{P,k}(O) &= \frac{\indicator(Z \in S_k)}{\pi_k(X)} \big(YW - P_{1,k}(X)\big), \\ R_{p,k}(O) &= \frac{\indicator(Z \in S_k)}{\pi_k(X)} \big(W - p_k(X)\big). \end{align*} At the known structural boundary, set $R_{P,0}=R_{p,0}\equiv0$; neither residual involves $\pi_0$ or a conditional regression on $S_0$. The key pathwise derivative of $m$ with respect to $J_{\text{full},k}(X)$ is: \begin{equation*} \frac{\partial m}{\partial J_{\text{full},k}}(X) = \gamma_{\text{full},k}(X) + \sum_{j=1}^{l_k} \gamma_{j,k}(X)\, \indicator\!\big(q_{j-1,k}(X) < p_{k+1}(X) - J_{\text{full},k}(X) < q_{j,k}(X)\big), \end{equation*} which equals the weight function evaluated at the lower integration boundary. This has a natural interpretation: an infinitesimal increase in $J_{\text{full},k}$ extends the integration region $[p_{k+1} - J_{\text{full},k},\, p_{k+1}]$ to the left, and the marginal contribution is precisely $\omega$ at the boundary. \paragraph{Auxiliary indicators.} To state the orthogonal score for $\mathbb{E}[h_{j,k}^{-}(X)]$, we introduce the following notation. Let \begin{equation*} I_{j,k}^{-}(X) \coloneqq \indicator\!\big(q_{j,k}(X) > \max(q_{j-1,k}(X),\, p_{k+1}(X) - J_{\text{full},k}(X))\big) \end{equation*} be the active set indicator for $h_{j,k}^{-}$, i.e., $I_{j,k}^{-} = \indicator(h_{j,k}^{-} > 0)$. Define the boundary indicator \begin{equation*} D_{j,k}^{-}(X) \coloneqq \indicator\!\big(q_{j-1,k}(X) < p_{k+1}(X) - J_{\text{full},k}(X) < q_{j,k}(X)\big), \end{equation*} which equals one precisely when the lower integration boundary $p_{k+1} - J_{\text{full},k}$ falls strictly inside the sub-interval $(q_{j-1,k}, q_{j,k})$, and the dominance indicator \begin{equation*} E_{j,k}^{-}(X) \coloneqq \indicator\!\big(q_{j-1,k}(X) > p_{k+1}(X) - J_{\text{full},k}(X)\big), \end{equation*} which equals one when the inner $\max$ in $h_{j,k}^{-}$ selects $q_{j-1,k}$. The two terms on the right partition the active set: $I_{j,k}^{-} = D_{j,k}^{-} + E_{j,k}^{-} \cdot \indicator(q_{j,k} > q_{j-1,k})$ (the two indicators are mutually exclusive). \paragraph{Pathwise derivatives.} The pathwise derivatives of $h_{j,k}^{-}$ with respect to the nuisance parameters are as follows. Since $h_{j,k}^{-}$ depends on $P_{1,m}$ only through $J_{\text{full},k} = P_{1,k+1} - P_{1,k}$: \begin{equation*} \alpha_{P,k+1}^{(j,k)}(X) = D_{j,k}^{-}(X), \qquad \alpha_{P,k}^{(j,k)}(X) = -D_{j,k}^{-}(X), \qquad \alpha_{P,m}^{(j,k)} = 0 \;\text{for } m \notin \{k, k{+}1\}. \end{equation*} For the propensity scores, $p_{k+1}$ enters $h_{j,k}^{-}$ directly through the boundary $p_{k+1} - J_{\text{full},k}$, and $p_k$ enters only when $j = 1$ (since $q_{0,k} = p_k$): \begin{equation*} \alpha_{p,k+1}^{(j,k)}(X) = -D_{j,k}^{-}(X), \qquad \alpha_{p,k}^{(j,k)}(X) = \begin{cases} -E_{1,k}^{-}(X)\, I_{1,k}^{-}(X) & \text{if } j = 1, \\ 0 & \text{if } j \geqslant 2. \end{cases} \end{equation*} \paragraph{Score for $h_{j,k}^{-}(X)$.} The complete orthogonal score for $\mathbb{E}[h_{j,k}^{-}(X)]$ is: \begin{align*} \psi_{j,k}^{h}(O; \eta) &= h_{j,k}^{-}(X) + D_{j,k}^{-}(X)\,\big(R_{P,k+1}(O) - R_{P,k}(O)\big) \\ &\quad + \alpha_{p,k+1}^{(j,k)}(X)\, R_{p,k+1}(O) + \alpha_{p,k}^{(j,k)}(X)\, R_{p,k}(O) \\ &\quad + I_{j,k}^{-}(X) R_{q,j,k}(O) \\ &\quad - E_{j,k}^{-}(X)\, I_{j,k}^{-}(X) R_{q,j-1,k}(O). \end{align*} The third line corrects for the estimation of the upper boundary $q_{j,k}$ through instruments in $T_{j,k}$, and the fourth line corrects for the lower boundary $q_{j-1,k}$ through instruments in $T_{j-1,k}$; the latter matters only when $q_{j-1,k}$ dominates $p_{k+1} - J_{\text{full},k}$ in the inner $\max$ (i.e., $E_{j,k}^{-} = 1$). When $j = 1$, the lower boundary $q_{0,k} = p_k$ does not depend on individual instruments, so the fourth line vanishes and its correction is absorbed into $\alpha_{p,k}^{(1,k)}$. \paragraph{Product scores.} The product score for the fractional interval component is: \begin{align*} \psi_{j,k}^{\text{prod}}(O; \eta) &= \gamma_{j,k}(X)\, \psi_{j,k}^{h}(O; \eta) + h_{j,k}^{-}(X) \left(\indicator\Big(Z \in \bigcup_{l=j}^{l_k} T_{l,k}\Big) - \gamma_{j,k}(X)\right). \end{align*} For the full-interval component, the orthogonal score for $J_{\text{full},k}(X) = P_{1,k+1}(X) - P_{1,k}(X)$ is simply $\psi_{\text{full},k}^{J}(O; \eta) = J_{\text{full},k}(X) + R_{P,k+1}(O) - R_{P,k}(O)$, and the full-interval product score is: \begin{align*} \psi_{\text{full},k}^{\text{prod}}(O; \eta) &= \gamma_{\text{full},k}(X)\,\psi_{\text{full},k}^{J}(O; \eta) \\ &\quad + J_{\text{full},k}(X)\left(\indicator\Big(Z \in \bigcup_{m=k+1}^{K} \bigcup_{l=0}^{l_m} T_{l,m}\Big) - \indicator\Big(Z \in \bigcup_{m=k+1}^{K} S_m\Big) - \gamma_{\text{full},k}(X)\right). \end{align*} The trivial bound score $\psi_{\Delta_K}(O; \eta)$ is identical to the continuous outcome case (see \cref{app:scores_continuous}), since $\Delta_K(X)$ does not involve the quantile structure. → `binScoreFamily` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Helpers/ScoresBinary.lean:290`)
- definition of the paper: \begin{align}\label{eq:binary_score} \psi(O; \eta) &= \sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\bigg(\psi_{\text{full},k}^{\text{prod}}(O; \eta) + \sum_{j=1}^{l_k} \psi_{j,k}^{\text{prod}}(O; \eta)\bigg) + \psi_{\Delta_K}(O; \eta). \end{align} → `psiBin` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Helpers/ScoresBinary.lean:202`)
- definition of the paper: Let $\eta_0$ denote the vector of true nuisance parameter values, let $\theta_0 = \underline{\theta}_{\omega,1}$ → `trueNuisance` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Helpers/Scores.lean:213`)
- definition of the paper: Choose a deterministic threshold $\rho_n\downarrow0$ such that $n^{1/4}\rho_n\to\infty$. Define $\widehat S_0=\{z:\bar p_2(z)\leqslant\rho_n\}$ and remove the corresponding baseline vertices from the clustering graph. Form an undirected graph from the remaining baseline vertices and all policy vertices. Connect two vertices when the corresponding scalar values among $\{\bar p_2(z),\bar q_2(z):z\in\mathcal Z\}$ differ by less than $c_{\mathrm{gap}}/2$, and define the estimated positive score levels as the graph's connected components. The baseline sets $\widehat S_k$, $k\geqslant1$, collect instruments whose remaining $(p,z)$ vertices share a component, ordered by their component averages. The policy sets $\widehat T_{j,k}$ collect instruments whose $(q,z)$ vertices share a component and are indexed by the baseline bin containing that component average, using the half-open bins for $k<K$ and the closed final bin for $k=K$. → `recoverLevels` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Helpers/DMLProcedureCore.lean:201`)
- definition of the paper: Following the standard DML template, we randomly partition the sample into an auxiliary set $I_1$ and a main estimation set $I_2$. On $I_1$ we estimate the propensity score $p$, clip it to $[0,1]$, and set $\hat q=\phi(\cdot,\cdot,\hat p)$; using only the covariates in $I_2$, we average $\hat p$ and $\hat q$ over $X$ and recover the level sets $\widehat S_k$ and $\widehat T_{j,k}$ as connected components of the graph that joins two averaged values lying within $c_{\mathrm{gap}}/2$ of each other. Given those sets we fit the remaining nuisances on $I_1$---the interval boundaries $\hat p_k$, the probability weights $\hat\gamma$, $\hat\pi_k$, $\hat\tau_{j,k}$ and within-set shares $\hat a_{j,k}$, the boundary quantiles $\hat\nu_{j,k}$, and the conditional expectations $\hat M^{\pm}_{j,k}$, $\hat J^{\pm}_{j,k}$, $\hat J^{\pm}_{\mathrm{full},k}$---clipping each to its known range, and finally average the plug-in orthogonal scores over $I_2$ to form~(\ref{eq:estimator_dml}). \cref{alg:dml_continuous_outcome} states the procedure step by step; the explicit graph construction, the moment equation defining $\nu_{j,k}$, the conditional-expectation components, and the Neyman-orthogonal scores $\psi^{\mathrm{prod}}$ are given in \cref{app:procedure_continuous_outcome} and \cref{app:scores_continuous}. → `IsDMLProcedure` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Helpers/DMLProcedure.lean:223`)
- condition of the paper: Randomly partition the observed dataset of size $n$ into two disjoint sets: an auxiliary sample $I_1$ and a main estimation sample $I_2$, each of size $n/2$. → `EqualHalfSplit` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Helpers/DMLProcedureCore.lean:52`)
- definition of the paper: Define $q_{j,i}(x)$ as the $j$-th smallest value of the alternative policy propensity $q(z,x)$ that falls in the baseline bin $[p_i(x), p_{i+1}(x))$ for $i<K$ and in the closed final bin $[p_K(x),1]$ for $i=K$, ordered as follows: \begin{equation*} p_i(x) \leqslant q_{1,i}(x) < \cdots < q_{l_i, i}(x) < p_{i+1}(x)\quad(i<K), \end{equation*} with the boundary conventions $q_{0,i}(x) = p_i(x)$ and $q_{l_i+1,i}(x) = p_{i+1}(x)$. Let the baseline and alternative instrument level sets be \begin{align*} S_k = \{z \in \mathcal{Z} : p(z,x) = p_k(x)\}, \quad T_{j,k} = \{z \in \mathcal{Z} : q(z,x) = q_{j,k}(x)\}, \quad j=1,\ldots,l_k. \end{align*} For the final bin, the last displayed strict inequality is replaced by $q_{l_K,K}(x)\leqslant p_{K+1}(x)=1$. Here $q_{0,i}$ and $q_{l_i+1,i}$ are integration boundaries; set $T_{0,i}=T_{l_i+1,i}=\emptyset$. Thus, if $q_{1,i}=p_i$, instruments attaining that value belong only to $T_{1,i}$, while an alternative propensity equal to one belongs to $T_{l_K,K}$ and is retained in the tail term. Values of $u$ exactly on a boundary are Lebesgue-null and do not affect the target integral. → `LevelStructure` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Helpers/DiscreteIV.lean:55`)
- definition of the paper: Estimating these five requires three further level-set quantities that do not appear in the representation itself---the tail weight $\gamma_K(x)\coloneqq\mathbb{P}_{\text{obs}}(Z\in\bigcup_{j=1}^{l_K}T_{j,K}\mid X=x)$, the within-set assignment shares $a_{j,k}(z,x)\coloneqq\mathbb{P}_{\text{obs}}(Z=z\mid Z\in T_{j,k},X=x)$, and the pooled representation $q_{j,k}(x)=\sum_{z\in T_{j,k}}a_{j,k}(z,x)q(z,x)$ that extends the policy level regularly when estimated instrument-specific levels are not exactly tied. → `levelShareFamily` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Helpers/DiscreteIV.lean:365`)
- definition of the paper: Let $\pi_k(x) \coloneqq \mathbb{P}_{\text{obs}}(Z \in S_k \mid X=x)$ and $\tau_{j,k}(x) \coloneqq \mathbb{P}_{\text{obs}}(Z \in T_{j,k} \mid X=x)$ denote the conditional probabilities of the baseline and alternative-policy level sets. → `levelSetProb` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Helpers/DiscreteIV.lean:143`)
- definition of the paper: the nuisances are $p_k$, $P_{1,k}$, $\pi_k$, $\tau_{j,k}$, $a_{j,k}$, $\gamma_{\mathrm{full},k}$, $\gamma_{j,k}$, and $\gamma_K$. → `NuisanceBin` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Helpers/ScoresBinary.lean:42`)
- definition of the paper: Thus, our target weight function simplifies to \begin{align*} \omega(x,u) \equiv \mathbb{E}_{\text{obs}}[\indicator(u \leqslant q(Z,X)) - \indicator(u \leqslant p(Z,X)) \mid X=x]. \end{align*} → `policyWeight` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Basic.lean:701`)
- definition of the paper: We model the alternative policy as $q(z,x) = \phi(z,x,p(z,x))$, where the known map $\phi:\mathcal Z\times\mathcal X\times[0,1]\to[0,1]$ is jointly measurable. → `policyProp` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Basic.lean:672`)
- definition of the paper: Throughout, we observe an i.i.d. sample $\{O_i\}_{i=1}^n$ of $O=(X,Z,W,Y)\sim\mathbb{P}_{\text{obs}}$, and $\eta$ collects the nuisance functions entering the bound, with $\hat\eta$ its estimate. → `ObservedSample` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Helpers/DMLProcedureCore.lean:33`)
- definition of the paper: The nuisance-estimation procedure mirrors \cref{alg:dml_continuous_outcome} with the conditional-quantile and indicator-expectation steps removed, and with one addition: because the $\hat P_{1,k}$ are fitted on different instrument subsets, they need not be monotone in $k$ in finite samples, so they are clipped to $[0,1]$ and rearranged before $\hat J_{\mathrm{full},k}=\hat P^*_{1,k+1}-\hat P^*_{1,k}$ is formed. \cref{alg:dml_binary_outcome} states the procedure and \cref{app:procedure_binary_outcome} and \cref{app:scores_discrete} give the full details and the orthogonal scores. → `rearrangeP1` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Helpers/DMLProcedureCore.lean:474`)
- definition of the paper: let $\mathcal{T}$ be the set of nuisance directions $\delta$ such that $\eta_0 + t\,\delta$ is an admissible nuisance value for all $t$ in a neighborhood of $0$. → `IsAdmissibleDirection` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Helpers/ScoresBinary.lean:281`)
- definition of the paper: and the total Neyman-orthogonal score: \begin{equation*} \psi(O;\eta) = \sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\Bigl(\psi_{\mathrm{full},k}^{\mathrm{prod}}(O;\eta) + \sum_{j=1}^{l_k}\psi_{j,k}^{\mathrm{prod}}(O;\eta)\Bigr) + \psi_{\Delta_K}(O;\eta), \end{equation*} → `psiCont` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Helpers/Scores.lean:412`)
- condition of the paper: Thus $\hat a_{j,k}(z,x)\geqslant0$ and $\sum_{z\in\widehat T_{j,k}}\hat a_{j,k}(z,x)=1$. → `FittedSharesSimplex` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Helpers/DMLProcedureCore.lean:569`)
- definition of the paper: The first two are the aggregate weights that the step weight $\omega$ assigns to instruments, \begin{align*} \gamma_{\text{full},k}(x) &\coloneqq \mathbb{P}_{\text{obs}}\!\left(Z \in \bigcup_{i=k+1}^K \bigcup_{l=0}^{l_i} T_{l,i} \mathrel{\Big|} X=x\right) - \mathbb{P}_{\text{obs}}\!\left(Z \in \bigcup_{i=k+1}^K S_i \mathrel{\Big|} X=x\right), \\ \gamma_{j,k}(x) &\coloneqq \mathbb{P}_{\text{obs}}\!\left(Z \in \bigcup_{l=j}^{l_k} T_{l,k} \mathrel{\Big|} X=x\right), \end{align*} where $\gamma_{\mathrm{full},k}\in[-1,1]$ because it differences two conditional probabilities. The next two carry the outcome distribution: with the relative threshold $\kappa_{j,k}(x) \coloneqq (q_{j,k}(x) - p_k(x))/(p_{k+1}(x) - p_k(x)) \in [0,1]$, \begin{align*} J_{\text{full},k}(x) &\coloneqq (p_{k+1}(x)-p_k(x))\,\mathbb{E}_{\mu_{1,k\mid x}}[Y], \\ J_{j,k}(x) &\coloneqq (p_{k+1}(x)-p_k(x))\int_{\kappa_{j,k}(x)}^{\kappa_{j+1,k}(x)} Q_{Y,k\mid x}(u)\,\mathrm{d}u, \end{align*} where $\mu_{1,k\mid x}$ is the identified conditional complier distribution of (\ref{eq:gap_identified_measure_cov}) and $Q_{Y,k\mid x}$ is its quantile function. The fifth is the trivial-bound boundary term \begin{equation*} \Delta_K(x) \coloneqq y_{\min}\, \mathbb{E}_{\text{obs}}\!\left[ \indicator\!\Big(Z \in \bigcup_{j=1}^{l_K} T_{j,K}\Big) (q(Z,X) - p_K(X)) \mathrel{\Big|} X=x \right]. \end{equation*} → `discreteComponents` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Helpers/DiscreteIV.lean:229`)
- definition of the paper: The Neyman-orthogonal score construction additionally requires the conditional indicator expectations \begin{align*} M_{j,k}^{+}(x) &\coloneqq \mathbb{E}[\indicator(Y \leqslant \nu_{j,k}(x)) \mid Z \in S_{k+1}, W=1, X=x], \\ M_{j,k}^{-}(x) &\coloneqq \mathbb{E}[\indicator(Y \leqslant \nu_{j,k}(x)) \mid Z \in S_k, W=1, X=x], \end{align*} → `boundaryIndicatorMean` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Helpers/Scores.lean:191`)
- definition of the paper: \begin{align*} J_{j,k}^{+}(X) & =\mathbb{E}[ Y\indicator( \nu _{j,k}( X) < Y \leqslant \nu _{j+1,k}( X)) \mid X,Z\in S_{k+1} ,W=1],\\ J_{j,k}^{-}(X) & =\mathbb{E}[ Y\indicator( \nu _{j,k}( X) < Y \leqslant \nu _{j+1,k}( X)) \mid X,Z\in S_{k} ,W=1] ,\\ J_{j,k}(X) & = p_{k+1}( X)J_{j,k}^{+}(X) - p_{k}( X)J_{j,k}^{-}(X), \end{align*} and estimate $ J_{\text{full} ,k} (X) $ by \begin{align*} J_{\text{full} ,k}^{+} (X) & =\mathbb{E}[ Y\mid X,Z\in S_{k+1} ,W=1] ,\\ J_{\text{full} ,k}^{-} (X) & =\mathbb{E}[ Y\mid X,Z\in S_{k} ,W=1] ,\\ J_{\text{full} ,k} (X) & = p_{k+1}( X)J_{\text{full} ,k}^{+} (X)- p_{k}( X)J_{\text{full} ,k}^{-} (X). \end{align*} → `treatedMeanFamily` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Helpers/Scores.lean:511`)
- definition of the paper: Set the lower boundary $\nu_{0,k}(x)\equiv y_{\min}$, and let $\nu_{j,k}(x) \coloneqq Q_{Y,k\mid x}(\kappa_{j,k}(x))$ for $j \in \{1,\dots,l_k\}$. → `bandBoundary` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Helpers/DiscreteIV.lean:282`)
- definition of the paper: \STATE Split sample into $I_1$ (auxiliary) and $I_2$ (main). \STATE \textbf{On $I_1$:} estimate propensity $\hat p(z,x)$, clip it to $[0,1]$, and set $\hat q(z,x) = \phi(z,x,\hat p(z,x))$. \STATE \textbf{Using the covariates in $I_2$:} form $\bar p_2(z)=|I_2|^{-1}\sum_{i\in I_2}\hat p(z,X_i)$ and $\bar q_2(z)=|I_2|^{-1}\sum_{i\in I_2}\hat q(z,X_i)$. Assign structural-zero categories to $\widehat S_0$ as in Step~1 below and remove their baseline vertices. Connect two remaining labeled values when their distance is less than $c_{\mathrm{gap}}/2$ and take graph connected components to obtain and order the positive-baseline sets $\widehat S_k$ and the policy sets $\widehat T_{j,k}$. \STATE \textbf{On $I_1$, given the recovered sets:} set $\hat p_0\equiv0$ and, for $k\geqslant1$, estimate $\hat p_k(x)=\widehat{\mathbb E}[W\mid Z\in\widehat S_k,X=x]$. Estimate the two conditional probabilities defining $\gamma_{\mathrm{full},k}(x)$ from their set indicators and subtract them; estimate $\hat\pi_k(x)$, $\hat\tau_{j,k}(x)$, $\hat\gamma_{j,k}(x)$, and $\hat\gamma_K(x)$ from the corresponding set indicators. Clip all estimated conditional probabilities to $[0,1]$ and $\hat\pi_k,\hat\tau_{j,k}$ further to $[c_\pi/2,1]$. Within each $\widehat T_{j,k}$, estimate the shares $\{\hat a_{j,k}(z,x):z\in\widehat T_{j,k}\}$ jointly with a simplex-valued multinomial learner, so they are nonnegative and sum to one, and set $\hat q_{j,k}(x)=\sum_{z\in\widehat T_{j,k}}\hat a_{j,k}(z,x)\hat q(z,x)$. \STATE Estimate boundary quantiles $\hat\nu_{j,k}(x)$ via the moment-equation regression in Step~4 below and clip them to $[y_{\min},y_{\max}]$. \STATE Estimate conditional expectations $\hat M_{j,k}^{\pm}(x)$, $\hat J_{j,k}^{\pm}(x)$, $\hat J_{\mathrm{full},k}^{\pm}(x)$ by flexible ML, omitting every minus-side object at the structural boundary $k=0$. \STATE \textbf{On $I_2$:} compute orthogonal scores $\hat\psi^{\mathrm{prod}}_{\mathrm{full},k}, \hat\psi^{\mathrm{prod}}_{j,k}, \hat\psi_{\Delta_K}$ from \cref{app:scores_continuous}. \STATE Return $\hat{\underline\theta}_{\omega,1}$ as in~(\ref{eq:estimator_dml}) and $\hat\sigma^2 = |I_2|^{-1}\sum_{i\in I_2}(\hat\psi(O_i;\hat\eta) - \hat{\underline\theta}_{\omega,1})^2$. → `IsDMLContinuousAlgorithm` (`lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/Helpers/DMLProcedure.lean:203`)

## Proposition 4.8

**Statement.** \label{prop:continuous_reduction} Suppose \cref{asp:structure} and \cref{asp:continuity_p} hold, and $\mathcal{Z}$ is connected. Then the sharp lower bound in \cref{thm:covariate_continuous_lower_bound} admits the representation \begin{equation} \label{eq:continuous_simplified_target} \begin{split} \underline{\theta}_{\omega, 1} &= \mathbb{E}_{Z,X} \Bigg[ - \int_{\min\{\underline{p}(X), q(Z,X)\}}^{\underline{p}(X)} Q_{1,\underline p\mid X}\!\left({v}/{\underline{p}(X)}\right) \, \mathrm{d}v \\ &\qquad + g_1\big(q^{\mathrm{tr}}(Z,X), X\big) - g_1\big(P, X\big) \\ &\qquad + y_{\min} \max\{0, q(Z,X) - \overline{p}(X)\} \Bigg], \end{split} \end{equation} where the first integral is defined as zero when $\underline{p}(X)=0$. The sharp upper bound is obtained by replacing $Q_{1,\underline p\mid X}(v/\underline{p}(X))$ with $Q_{1,\underline p\mid X}(1-v/\underline{p}(X))$ and $y_{\min}$ with $y_{\max}$.

**Lean.** `Papers.TbsPrteOptimalTransport.continuous_reduction` — `lean/Papers/TbsPrteOptimalTransport/TContinuousReduction.lean:30`
